<a href="https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Category:_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri" class="extiw" title="commons:Category: Lal Bahadur Shastri"><b>Gallery</b>: Picture, Sound, Video</a></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1129693374"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1063604349">.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar li{word-spacing:-0.125em}.mw-parser-output .navbar a>span,.mw-parser-output .navbar a>abbr{text-decoration:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-mini abbr{font-variant:small-caps;border-bottom:none;text-decoration:none;cursor:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-full{font-size:114%;margin:0 7em}.mw-parser-output .navbar-ct-mini{font-size:114%;margin:0 4em}</style><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Lal_Bahadur_Shastri" title="Template:Lal Bahadur Shastri"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Lal_Bahadur_Shastri" title="Template talk:Lal Bahadur Shastri"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Template:Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Lal Bahadur Shastri</b> (<small>pronounced&#160;</small><span class="IPA nowrap" lang="hi-Latn-fonipa" title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Hindi_and_Urdu" title="Help:IPA/Hindi and Urdu">[lɑːl<span class="wrap"> </span>bəˈhɑːd̪ʊɾ<span class="wrap"> </span>ˈʃɑːst̪ɾi]</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/5\/5b\/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri.ogg\/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;Lal Bahadur Shastri.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/5/5b/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri.ogg/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:Lal_Bahadur_Shastri.ogg" title="File:Lal Bahadur Shastri.ogg">i</a></sup>; 2 October 1904&#160;&#8211;&#32;11 January 1966) was an Indian politician and statesman who served as the second <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_India" title="Prime Minister of India">prime minister of India</a> from 1964 to 1966. He previously served as the sixth <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Home_Affairs_(India)" title="Minister of Home Affairs (India)">home minister of India</a> from 1961 to 1963. 
</p><p>Shastri was born to Sharada Prasad Srivastava and Ramdulari Devi in <a href="/wiki/Mughalsarai" title="Mughalsarai">Mughalsarai</a> on 2 October 1904. He studied in East Central Railway Inter college and Harish Chandra High School, which he left to join the <a href="/wiki/Non-cooperation_movement" title="Non-cooperation movement">non-cooperation movement</a>. He worked for the betterment of the <a href="/wiki/Harijan" class="mw-redirect" title="Harijan">Harijans</a> at <a href="/wiki/Muzaffarpur" title="Muzaffarpur">Muzaffarpur</a> and dropped his caste-derived surname of "Srivastava". Shastri's thoughts were influenced by reading about <a href="/wiki/Swami_Vivekananda" title="Swami Vivekananda">Swami Vivekananda</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mahatma_Gandhi" title="Mahatma Gandhi">Mahatma Gandhi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Annie_Besant" title="Annie Besant">Annie Besant</a>. Deeply impressed and influenced by Gandhi, he joined the <a href="/wiki/Indian_independence_movement" title="Indian independence movement">Indian Independence movement</a> in the 1920s. He served as the president of <a href="/wiki/Servants_of_the_People_Society" title="Servants of the People Society">Servants of the People Society</a> (Lok Sevak Mandal), founded by <a href="/wiki/Lala_Lajpat_Rai" title="Lala Lajpat Rai">Lala Lajpat Rai</a> and held prominent positions in the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Indian National Congress</a>. Following independence in 1947, he joined the Indian government and became one of <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister" class="mw-redirect" title="Prime Minister">Prime Minister</a> Nehru's key cabinet colleagues, first as <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Railways_(India)" title="Minister of Railways (India)">Railways Minister</a> (1951–56), and then in numerous other prominent positions, including the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Home_Affairs_(India)" title="Ministry of Home Affairs (India)">Home Minister</a>.
</p><p>As prime minister, Shastri promoted the <a href="/wiki/Operation_Flood" title="Operation Flood">White Revolution</a> – a national campaign to increase the production and supply of milk – by supporting the <a href="/wiki/Amul" title="Amul">Amul</a> milk co-operative of <a href="/wiki/Anand,_Gujarat" title="Anand, Gujarat">Anand, Gujarat</a> and creating the <a href="/wiki/National_Dairy_Development_Board" title="National Dairy Development Board">National Dairy Development Board</a>. Underlining the need to boost India's food production, Shastri also promoted the <a href="/wiki/Green_Revolution_in_India" title="Green Revolution in India">Green Revolution in India</a> in 1965. This led to an increase in food grain production, especially in the states of <a href="/wiki/Punjab" title="Punjab">Punjab</a>, <a href="/wiki/Haryana" title="Haryana">Haryana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh" title="Uttar Pradesh">Uttar Pradesh</a>. He led the country during the <a href="/wiki/Indo-Pakistan_War_of_1965" class="mw-redirect" title="Indo-Pakistan War of 1965">Second India–Pakistan War</a>. His slogan "<a href="/wiki/Jai_Jawan_Jai_Kisan" title="Jai Jawan Jai Kisan">Jai Jawan, Jai Kisan</a>" ("Hail to the soldier; Hail to the farmer") became very popular during the war. The war formally ended with the <a href="/wiki/Tashkent_Declaration" title="Tashkent Declaration">Tashkent Declaration</a> on 10 January 1966; Shastri died the next day.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span id="Early_years_.281904.E2.80.931920.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_years_(1904–1920)">Early years (1904–1920)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early years (1904–1920)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Shastri was born on 2 October 1904 at the home of his maternal grandparents in a Kayastha family.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> Shastri's paternal ancestors were in the service of the <i><a href="/wiki/Zamindar" title="Zamindar">zamindar</a></i> of <a href="/wiki/Ramnagar,_Varanasi" title="Ramnagar, Varanasi">Ramnagar</a> near <a href="/wiki/Varanasi" title="Varanasi">Banaras</a>, and Shastri lived there for the first year of his life. Shastri's father, Sharada Prasad Verma, was a school teacher who later became a clerk in the revenue office at <a href="/wiki/Prayagraj" title="Prayagraj">Prayagraj</a>, while his mother, Ramdulari Devi, was the daughter of Munshi Hazari Lal, the headmaster and English teacher at a railway school in Mughalsarai. Shastri was the second child and eldest son of his parents; he had an elder sister, Kailashi Devi (b. 1900).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBakshi19911,_2_4-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBakshi19911,_2-4">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDhawan199181_5-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDhawan199181-5">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In April 1906, when Shastri was hardly 18 months old, his father, who had only recently been promoted to the post of deputy tahsildar, died in an epidemic of <a href="/wiki/Bubonic_plague" title="Bubonic plague">bubonic plague</a>. Ramdulari Devi, then only 23 years old and pregnant with her third child, took her two children and moved from Ramnagar to her father's house in Mughalsarai and settled there for good. She gave birth to a daughter, Sundari Devi, in July 1906.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEC.P._Srivastava199512–17_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEC.P._Srivastava199512–17-6">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-pmindia_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pmindia-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Thus, Shastri and his sisters grew up in the household of his maternal grandfather, Hazari Lalji.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> However, Hazari Lalji himself died from a <a href="/wiki/Stroke" title="Stroke">stroke</a> in mid-1908. Thereafter, the family was looked after by his brother (Shastri's great-uncle) Darbari Lal, who was the head clerk in the opium regulation department at Ghazipur, and later by his son (Ramdulari Devi's cousin) Bindeshwari Prasad, a school teacher in Mughalsarai.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEChokkan20209_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChokkan20209-9">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>This situation was perfectly in tune with the milieu of the times, where the Indian <a href="/wiki/Joint_family" class="mw-redirect" title="Joint family">Joint family</a> system was a thriving reality; the sense of family relationship and responsibility it fostered was the primary social security of the time. Nor should be surmised from these circumstances that Shastri grew up in an under-privileged manner, or that his education and comforts were compromised in any manner. On the contrary, since he was a rank student, he received a better education than some of his cousins.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEC.P._Srivastava1995_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEC.P._Srivastava1995-10">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> and Bindeshwari Prasad, on the limited salary of a school teacher, with many dependents, nevertheless managed to give a good education to all the children in his care.
</p><p>In 1917, Bindeshwari Prasad was transferred to Varanasi, and the entire family moved there, including Ramdulari Devi and her three children. In Varanasi, Shastri joining the seventh standard at Harish Chandra High School.
</p>
<h2><span id="Gandhi.27s_disciple_.281921.E2.80.931945.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Gandhi's_disciple_(1921–1945)">Gandhi's disciple (1921–1945)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Gandhi&#039;s disciple (1921–1945)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>While his family had no links to the independence movement then taking shape, among his teachers at Harish Chandra High School was an intensely patriotic and highly respected teacher named Nishkameshwar Prasad Mishra, who gave Shastri much-needed financial support by allowing him to tutor his children. Inspired by Mishra's patriotism, Shastri took a deep interest in the freedom struggle, and began to study its history and the works of several of its noted personalities, including those of Swami Vivekananda, Mahatma Gandhi and Annie Besant.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAnkit20167_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnkit20167-11">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> In January 1921, when Shastri was in the 10th standard and three months from sitting the final examinations, he attended a public meeting in <a href="/wiki/Benares" class="mw-redirect" title="Benares">Benares</a> hosted by Gandhi and <a href="/wiki/Pandit_Madan_Mohan_Malaviya" class="mw-redirect" title="Pandit Madan Mohan Malaviya">Pandit Madan Mohan Malaviya</a>. Inspired by the Mahatma's call for students to withdraw from government schools and join the non-cooperation movement, Shastri withdrew from Harish Chandra High School the next day and joined the local branch of the Congress Party as a volunteer, actively participating in picketing and anti-government demonstrations.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> He was soon arrested and jailed, but was then let off as he was still a minor.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEC.P._Srivastava199520–22_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEC.P._Srivastava199520–22-14">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Inauguration_of_MNREC_Building_Allahabad_by_Indian_PM_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shashtri.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Inauguration_of_MNREC_Building_Allahabad_by_Indian_PM_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shashtri.JPG/220px-Inauguration_of_MNREC_Building_Allahabad_by_Indian_PM_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shashtri.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="371" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Inauguration_of_MNREC_Building_Allahabad_by_Indian_PM_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shashtri.JPG/330px-Inauguration_of_MNREC_Building_Allahabad_by_Indian_PM_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shashtri.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Inauguration_of_MNREC_Building_Allahabad_by_Indian_PM_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shashtri.JPG/440px-Inauguration_of_MNREC_Building_Allahabad_by_Indian_PM_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shashtri.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1448" data-file-height="2443" /></a><figcaption>Inauguration of the Main Building of MNREC Allahabad by Indian prime minister Shri Lal Bahadur Shashtri on 18 April 1965</figcaption></figure>
<p>Shastri's immediate supervisor was a former <a href="/wiki/Benares_Hindu_University" class="mw-redirect" title="Benares Hindu University">Benares Hindu University</a> lecturer named <a href="/wiki/J.B._Kripalani" class="mw-redirect" title="J.B. Kripalani">J.B. Kripalani</a>, who would become one of the most prominent leaders of the Indian independence movement and one among Gandhi's closest followers.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> Recognising the need for the younger volunteers to continue their educations, Kripalani and a friend, V.N. Sharma, had founded an informal school centered around "nationalist education" to educate the young activists in their nation's heritage and with the support of a wealthy philanthropist and ardent Congress nationalist, Shiv Prasad Gupta, the <a href="/wiki/Kashi_Vidyapith" class="mw-redirect" title="Kashi Vidyapith">Kashi Vidyapith</a> was inaugurated by Gandhi in Benares as a national institution of higher education on 10 February 1921. Among the first students of the new institution, Shastri graduated with a first-class degree in philosophy and ethics from the Vidyapith in 1925. He was given the title <i><a href="/wiki/Shastri" title="Shastri">Shastri</a></i> ("scholar").<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The title was a bachelor's degree awarded by the institution but it stuck as part of his name.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEC.P._Srivastava199523–28_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEC.P._Srivastava199523–28-17">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Shastri enrolled himself as a life member of the <a href="/wiki/Servants_of_the_People_Society" title="Servants of the People Society">Servants of the People Society</a> (Lok Sevak Mandal), founded by <a href="/wiki/Lala_Lajpat_Rai" title="Lala Lajpat Rai">Lala Lajpat Rai</a>, and began to work for the betterment of the <a href="/wiki/Harijan" class="mw-redirect" title="Harijan">Harijans</a> under Gandhi's direction at <a href="/wiki/Muzaffarpur" title="Muzaffarpur">Muzaffarpur</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-rrtd_lbs_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rrtd_lbs-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> Later he became the President of the Society.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGrover1993547–_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrover1993547–-21">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Independence_Activism_of_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri">Independence Activism of Lal Bahadur Shastri</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Independence Activism of Lal Bahadur Shastri">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1928 Shastri became an active and mature member of the Indian National Congress at the call of Mahatma Gandhi. He was imprisoned for two and a half years.<sup id="cite_ref-freeindia_freedom_soldier_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-freeindia_freedom_soldier-22">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Later, he worked as the Organizing Secretary of the Parliamentary Board of <a href="/wiki/United_Provinces_of_Agra_and_Oudh" title="United Provinces of Agra and Oudh">U.P.</a> in 1937.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> In 1940, he was sent to prison for one year, for offering individual <a href="/wiki/Satyagraha" title="Satyagraha">Satyagraha</a> support to the independence movement.<sup id="cite_ref-freeindia_prison_again_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-freeindia_prison_again-24">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 8 August 1942, Mahatma Gandhi issued the <a href="/wiki/Quit_India_speech" title="Quit India speech">Quit India speech</a> at <a href="/wiki/Gowalia_Tank" title="Gowalia Tank">Gowalia Tank</a> in <a href="/wiki/Bombay" class="mw-redirect" title="Bombay">Bombay</a>, demanding that the British leave India. Shastri, who had just then come out after a year in prison, travelled to <a href="/wiki/Allahabad" class="mw-redirect" title="Allahabad">Allahabad</a>. For a week, he sent instructions to the independence activists from <a href="/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru" title="Jawaharlal Nehru">Jawaharlal Nehru</a>'s home, <a href="/wiki/Anand_Bhavan" title="Anand Bhavan">Anand Bhavan</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> He served as an elected representative for <a href="/wiki/United_Provinces_(1937%E2%80%9350)" class="mw-redirect" title="United Provinces (1937–50)">United Provinces</a> in <a href="/wiki/1937_Indian_provincial_elections" title="1937 Indian provincial elections">1937</a> and <a href="/wiki/1946_Indian_provincial_elections" title="1946 Indian provincial elections">1946</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Political_Career_.281947.E2.80.931964.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_Career_(1947–1964)">Political Career (1947–1964)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Political Career (1947–1964)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="State_minister">State minister</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: State minister">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Following India's independence, Shastri was appointed Parliamentary Secretary in his home state, <a href="/wiki/Uttar_Pradesh" title="Uttar Pradesh">Uttar Pradesh</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERajeshwar_Prasad199123_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERajeshwar_Prasad199123-27">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> He became the Minister of Police and Transport under <a href="/wiki/Govind_Ballabh_Pant" title="Govind Ballabh Pant">Govind Ballabh Pant</a>'s Chief Ministership on 15 August 1947 following <a href="/wiki/Rafi_Ahmed_Kidwai" title="Rafi Ahmed Kidwai">Rafi Ahmed Kidwai</a>'s departure to become a minister at the centre. As the Transport Minister, he was the first to appoint women <a href="/wiki/Conductor_(transportation)" class="mw-redirect" title="Conductor (transportation)">conductors</a>. As the minister in charge of the Police Department, he ordered that police use water jets, whose instructions was given by him, instead of <a href="/wiki/Lathi_charge" class="mw-redirect" title="Lathi charge">lathis</a> to disperse unruly crowds.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> His tenure as police minister (As Home Minister was called prior to 1950) saw successful curbing of communal riots in 1947, mass migration and resettlement of refugees.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cabinet_minister">Cabinet minister</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Cabinet minister">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Cabinet_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Cabinet of India">Cabinet of India</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Jawaharlal_Nehru_with_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_and_K._Kamaraj.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Jawaharlal_Nehru_with_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_and_K._Kamaraj.jpg/220px-Jawaharlal_Nehru_with_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_and_K._Kamaraj.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="238" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Jawaharlal_Nehru_with_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_and_K._Kamaraj.jpg/330px-Jawaharlal_Nehru_with_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_and_K._Kamaraj.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Jawaharlal_Nehru_with_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_and_K._Kamaraj.jpg/440px-Jawaharlal_Nehru_with_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_and_K._Kamaraj.jpg 2x" data-file-width="452" data-file-height="488" /></a><figcaption>Jawaharlal Nehru with Lal Bahadur Shastri and K. Kamaraj</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 1951, Shastri was made the General Secretary of the <a href="/wiki/All_India_Congress_Committee" title="All India Congress Committee">All-India Congress Committee</a> with Jawaharlal Nehru as the prime minister. He was directly responsible for the selection of candidates and the direction of publicity and electioneering activities. He played an important role in the landslide successes of the Congress Party in the Indian <a href="/wiki/General_Elections" class="mw-redirect" title="General Elections">General Elections</a> of 1952, 1957 and 1962. In 1952, he successfully contested UP Vidhansabha from Soraon North cum Phulpur West seat and won by getting over 69% of vote. He was believed to be retained as home minister of UP, but in a surprise move was called to Centre as minister by Nehru. Shastri was made <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Railways_(India)" title="Minister of Railways (India)">Minister of Railways</a> and <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Transport_(India)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister of Transport (India)">Transport</a> in <a href="/wiki/First_Cabinet_of_India_1952" class="mw-redirect" title="First Cabinet of India 1952">First Cabinet</a> of <a href="/wiki/Republic_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Republic of India">Republic of India</a> on 13 May 1952.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In September 1956 he wanted to take political and moral responsibility for the <a href="/wiki/1956_Mahbubnagar_train_accident" title="1956 Mahbubnagar train accident">1956 Mahbubnagar train accident</a> and offered his resignation as the Minister of Railways to prime minister <a href="/wiki/Jawaharlal_Nehru" title="Jawaharlal Nehru">Jawaharlal Nehru</a>, but she refused. After the <a href="/wiki/1956_Ariyalur_train_accident" title="1956 Ariyalur train accident">1956 Ariyalur train accident</a>, a similar accident about 2.5 months later, Shastri again offered his resignation and was accepted this time.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> He resigned as Railway minister on 7 December 1956.<sup id="cite_ref-NIE_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-NIE-33">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>He served as the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Commerce_and_Industry_(India)" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister of Commerce and Industry (India)">Minister of Commerce and Industry</a> in 1959 and <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Home_Affairs_(India)" title="Minister of Home Affairs (India)">Minister of Home Affairs</a> in 1961.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERajeshwar_Prasad199111-12_34-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERajeshwar_Prasad199111-12-34">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Shastri laid the foundation of <a href="/wiki/New_Mangalore_Port" title="New Mangalore Port">Mangalore Port</a> in 1964 as a minister without a portfolio.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERajeshwar_Prasad19915_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERajeshwar_Prasad19915-35">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span id="Prime_minister_.281964.E2.80.931966.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Prime_minister_(1964–1966)">Prime minister (1964–1966)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Prime minister (1964–1966)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Premiership_of_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri" title="Premiership of Lal Bahadur Shastri">Premiership of Lal Bahadur Shastri</a></div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_ministry" class="mw-redirect" title="Lal Bahadur Shastri ministry">Lal Bahadur Shastri ministry</a></div>
<p>Jawaharlal Nehru died in office on 27 May 1964. Then Congress Party president <a href="/wiki/K._Kamaraj" title="K. Kamaraj">K. Kamaraj</a> was instrumental in making Shastri prime minister on 9 June. Shastri, though mild-mannered and soft-spoken, was a Nehruvian <a href="/wiki/Socialist" class="mw-redirect" title="Socialist">socialist</a> and thus held appeal to those wishing to prevent the ascent of conservative right-winger <a href="/wiki/Morarji_Desai" title="Morarji Desai">Morarji Desai</a>.
</p><p>In his first broadcast as prime minister, on 11 June 1964, Shastri stated:<sup id="cite_ref-pib_might_of_peace_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pib_might_of_peace-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>There comes a time in the life of every nation when it stands at the cross-roads of history and must choose which way to go. But for us, there need be no difficulty or hesitation, no looking to right or left. Our way is straight and clear—the building up of a socialist democracy at home with freedom and prosperity for all, and the maintenance of world peace and friendship with all nations.</p></blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Domestic_policies">Domestic policies</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Domestic policies">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Shastri retained many members of Nehru's <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Ministers_of_the_Republic_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Council of Ministers of the Republic of India">Council of Ministers</a>. <a href="/wiki/T._T._Krishnamachari" title="T. T. Krishnamachari">T. T. Krishnamachari</a> was retained as the <a href="/wiki/Finance_Minister_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Finance Minister of India">Finance Minister of India</a>, as was <a href="/wiki/Defence_Minister_of_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Defence Minister of India">Defence Minister</a> <a href="/wiki/Yashwantrao_Chavan" title="Yashwantrao Chavan">Yashwantrao Chavan</a>. He appointed <a href="/wiki/Swaran_Singh" title="Swaran Singh">Swaran Singh</a> to succeed him as <a href="/wiki/External_Affairs_Minister" class="mw-redirect" title="External Affairs Minister">External Affairs Minister</a>. He also appointed <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a>, daughter of Jawaharlal Nehru and former Congress President, as the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Information_and_Broadcasting" class="mw-redirect" title="Minister of Information and Broadcasting">Minister of Information and Broadcasting</a>. Gulzarilal Nanda continued as the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Home_Affairs_(India)" title="Ministry of Home Affairs (India)">Minister of Home Affairs</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Anti-Hindi_agitations_of_Tamil_Nadu" title="Anti-Hindi agitations of Tamil Nadu">Anti-Hindi agitations of Tamil Nadu</a></div>
<p>Lal Bahadur Shastri's tenure witnessed the Madras anti-Hindi agitation of 1965. The government of India had for a long time made an effort to establish <a href="/wiki/Hindi" title="Hindi">Hindi</a> as the sole national language of India. This was resisted by the non-Hindi speaking states particularly <a href="/wiki/Madras_State" title="Madras State">Madras State</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> To calm the situation, Shastri gave assurances that English would continue to be used as the official language as long the non-Hindi speaking states wanted. The riots subsided after Shastri's assurance, as did the student agitation.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuha2008187-189_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuha2008187-189-39">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Economic_policies">Economic policies</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Economic policies">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Shastri continued Nehru's socialist economic policies with central planning.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBakshi199149_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBakshi199149-40">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> He promoted the <a href="/wiki/Operation_Flood" title="Operation Flood">White Revolution</a> – a national campaign to increase the production and supply of milk – by supporting the <a href="/wiki/Amul" title="Amul">Amul</a> milk co-operative of <a href="/wiki/Anand,_Gujarat" title="Anand, Gujarat">Anand, Gujarat</a> and creating the <a href="/wiki/National_Dairy_Development_Board" title="National Dairy Development Board">National Dairy Development Board</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-pib_text9089_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pib_text9089-41">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> He visited Anand on 31 October 1964 for inauguration of the Cattle Feed Factory of Amul at Kanjari. As he was keenly interested in knowing the success of this co-operative, he stayed overnight with farmers in a village, and even had dinner with a farmer's family. He discussed his wish with <a href="/wiki/Verghese_Kurien" title="Verghese Kurien">Verghese Kurien</a>, then the General Manager of Kaira District Co-operative Milk Producers' Union Ltd (Amul) to replicate this model to other parts of the country for improving the socio-economic conditions of farmers. As a result of this visit, the National Dairy Development Board (NDDB) was established at Anand in 1965.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>While speaking on the chronic food shortages across the country, Shastri urged people to voluntarily give up one meal so that the food saved could be distributed to the affected populace. However, he ensured that he first implemented the system in his own family before appealing to the country. He went on air to appeal to his countrymen to skip a meal a week. The response to his appeal was overwhelming. Even restaurants and eateries downed the shutters on Monday evenings. Many parts of the country observed the "Shastri Vrat". He motivated the country to maximize the cultivation of food grains by ploughing the lawn himself, at his official residence in New Delhi. During the 22-day war with Pakistan in 1965, On 19 October 1965, Shastri gave the seminal 'Jai Jawan Jai Kishan' ("Hail the soldier, Hail the farmer") slogan at Urwa in Allahabad that became a national slogan. Underlining the need to boost India's food production, Shastri also promoted the <a href="/wiki/Green_Revolution_in_India" title="Green Revolution in India">Green Revolution in India</a> in 1965.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup> This led to an increase in food grain production, especially in <a href="/wiki/Punjab,_India" title="Punjab, India">Punjab</a>, <a href="/wiki/Haryana" title="Haryana">Haryana</a>, and Uttar Pradesh. Major milestones in this undertaking were the development of high-yielding varieties of <a href="/wiki/Wheat" title="Wheat">wheat</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-about_IARI_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-about_IARI-46">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Rust_(fungus)" title="Rust (fungus)">rust</a> resistant strains of wheat.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-47">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:DB-LBS.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/DB-LBS.png/220px-DB-LBS.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="162" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/DB-LBS.png/330px-DB-LBS.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2d/DB-LBS.png/440px-DB-LBS.png 2x" data-file-width="955" data-file-height="704" /></a><figcaption>Lal Bahadur Shastri in 1964</figcaption></figure>
<p>Though he was a socialist, Shastri stated that India cannot have a regimented type of economy. His government passed the <a href="/w/index.php?title=National_Agricultural_Products_Board_Act&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="National Agricultural Products Board Act (page does not exist)">National Agricultural Products Board Act</a> and was responsible for setting up the <a href="/wiki/Food_Corporation_of_India" title="Food Corporation of India">Food Corporation of India</a> under the Food Corporation's Act 1964.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Jai_Jawan_Jai_Kisan">Jai Jawan Jai Kisan</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Jai Jawan Jai Kisan">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Jai_Jawan_Jai_Kisan" title="Jai Jawan Jai Kisan">Jai Jawan Jai Kisan</a></div>
<p>For the outstanding <a href="/wiki/Slogan" title="Slogan">slogan</a> given by him during Indo-Pak war of 1965 <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Information_and_Broadcasting_(India)" title="Ministry of Information and Broadcasting (India)">Ministry of Information and Broadcasting (India)</a> commemorated Shastri even after 47 years of his death on his 48th martyr's day:<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>Former Prime Minister Lal Bahadur Shastri was one of those great Indians who has left an indelible impression on our collective life. Lal Bahadur Shastri's contribution to our public life were unique in that they were made in the closest proximity to the life of the common man in India. Lal Bahadur Shastri was looked upon by Indians as one of their own, one who shared their ideals, hopes and aspirations. His achievements were looked upon not as the isolated achievements of an individual but of our society collectively.
Under Shastri's leadership India faced and repulsed the Pakistani invasion of 1965. It is not only a matter of pride for the Indian Army but also for every citizen of the country. His slogan <b>Jai Jawan! Jai Kisan!!</b> reverberates even today through the length and breadth of the country. Underlying this is the inner-most sentiments 'Jai Hind'. The war of 1965 was fought and won for our self-respect and our national prestige. For using our Defence Forces with such admirable skill, the nation remains beholden to Shri Lal Bahadur Shastri. He will be remembered for all times to come for his large heartedness and public service.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup></p></blockquote>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_policies">Foreign policies</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Foreign policies">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Shastri continued Nehru's policy of <a href="/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Non-Aligned Movement">non-alignment</a> but also built closer relations with the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a>. In the aftermath of the <a href="/wiki/Sino-Indian_War" title="Sino-Indian War">Sino-Indian War</a> of 1962 and the formation of military ties between China and Pakistan, Shastri's government decided to expand the country's defence budget.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBakshi199123-26_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBakshi199123-26-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup>
In 1964, Shastri signed an accorresponsibilities of local governments to provide adequate facilities to shelter the repatriates upon disembarkation on Indian soil. Particularly in the Madras State the Chief Minister during that time, Minjur K. Bhaktavatsalam, showed care in rehabilitation of the returnees. In December 1965, Shastri made an official visit with his family to Rangoon, Burma and re-established cordial relations with the country's military government of General <a href="/wiki/Ne_Win" title="Ne Win">Ne Win</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="War_with_Pakistan">War with Pakistan</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: War with Pakistan">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Second_India%E2%80%93Pakistan_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Second India–Pakistan War">Second India–Pakistan War</a></div>
<p>Laying claim to half the <a href="/wiki/Kutch" class="mw-redirect" title="Kutch">Kutch peninsula</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Pakistan_Army_order_of_battle,_December_1971" title="Pakistan Army order of battle, December 1971">Pakistani army</a> skirmished with Indian forces in August 1965. In his report to the <a href="/wiki/Lok_Sabha" title="Lok Sabha">Lok Sabha</a> on the confrontation in <a href="/wiki/Kutch" class="mw-redirect" title="Kutch">Kutch</a>, Shastri stated:<sup id="cite_ref-pib_might_of_peace_36-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pib_might_of_peace-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-britannica_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>In the utilization of our limited resources, we have always given primacy to plans and projects for economic development. It would, therefore, be obvious for anyone who is prepared to look at things objectively that India can have no possible interest in provoking border incidents or in building up an atmosphere of strife... In these circumstances, the duty of Government is quite clear and this duty will be discharged fully and effectively... We would prefer to live in poverty for as long as necessary but we shall not allow our freedom to be subverted.</p></blockquote>
<p>On 1 August 1965, major incursions of militants and Pakistani soldiers began, hoping not only to break down the government but incite a sympathetic revolt. The revolt did not happen, and India sent its forces across the Ceasefire Line (now <a href="/wiki/Line_of_Control" title="Line of Control">Line of Control</a>) and threatened Pakistan by crossing the International Border near <a href="/wiki/Lahore" title="Lahore">Lahore</a> as war broke out on a general scale. Massive tank battles occurred in the <a href="/wiki/Punjab_region" class="mw-redirect" title="Punjab region">Punjab</a>, and while the Pakistani forces made gains in the northern part of subcontinent, Indian forces captured the key post at Haji Pir, in Kashmir, and brought the Pakistani city of <a href="/wiki/Lahore" title="Lahore">Lahore</a> under artillery and mortar fire.
</p><p>The Indo-Pak war ended on 23 September 1965 with a <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>-mandated ceasefire. In a broadcast to the nation on the day of the ceasefire, Shastri stated:<sup id="cite_ref-pib_might_of_peace_36-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pib_might_of_peace-36">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>While the conflict between the armed forces of the two countries has come to an end, the more important thing for the United Nations and all those who stand for peace is to bring to an end the deeper conflict.... How can this be brought about? In our view, the only answer lies in peaceful coexistence. India has stood for the principle of coexistence and championed it all over the world. Peaceful coexistence is possible among nations no matter how deep the differences between them, how far apart they are in their political and economic systems, no matter how intense the issues that divide them.</p></blockquote>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_in_1963.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_in_1963.jpg/220px-Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_in_1963.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="166" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_in_1963.jpg/330px-Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_in_1963.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_in_1963.jpg/440px-Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_in_1963.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="774" /></a><figcaption>Lal Bahadur Shastri with police officers</figcaption></figure>
<p>During his tenure as prime minister, Shastri visited many countries including the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a>, <a href="/wiki/Yugoslavia" title="Yugoslavia">Yugoslavia</a>, England, <a href="/wiki/Canada" title="Canada">Canada</a>, Nepal, Egypt and <a href="/wiki/Burma" class="mw-redirect" title="Burma">Burma</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-rrtd_lbs_19-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-rrtd_lbs-19">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> In October 1964 while returning from the Non Alliance Conference in Cairo, on the invitation of the-then president of Pakistan, <a href="/wiki/Muhammad_Ayub_Khan" title="Muhammad Ayub Khan">Muhammad Ayub Khan</a>, to have lunch with him, Shastri made a stopover at Karachi Airport for a few hours. Breaking with protocol, Ayub Khan personally received him at the airport and they had an informal meeting. 
</p><p>After the ceasefire with Pakistan in 1965, Shastri and Ayub Khan attended a summit in <a href="/wiki/Tashkent" title="Tashkent">Tashkent</a> (former <a href="/wiki/USSR" class="mw-redirect" title="USSR">USSR</a>, now in modern <a href="/wiki/Uzbekistan" title="Uzbekistan">Uzbekistan</a>), organized by <a href="/wiki/Alexei_Kosygin" title="Alexei Kosygin">Alexei Kosygin</a>. On 10 January 1966, Shastri and Ayub Khan signed the <a href="/wiki/Tashkent_Declaration" title="Tashkent Declaration">Tashkent Declaration</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-3">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Family_and_personal_life">Family and personal life</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Family and personal life">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Shastri was 5&#160;ft 2 inches tall<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> and always used to wear <i><a href="/wiki/Dhoti" title="Dhoti">dhoti</a></i>. The only occasion on which he wore <i>pyjamas</i> was dinner in honor of the Queen of the United Kingdom in 1961 in the Rashtrapati Bhavan.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERajeshwar_Prasad199111_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERajeshwar_Prasad199111-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> On 16 May 1928, Shastri married <a href="/wiki/Lalita_Shastri" title="Lalita Shastri">Lalita Devi</a> who was from <a href="/wiki/Mirzapur" title="Mirzapur">Mirzapur</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The couple had four sons and two daughters, namely Kusum Shastri, the eldest daughter, <a href="/wiki/Hari_Krishna_Shastri" title="Hari Krishna Shastri">Hari Krishna Shastri</a>, the eldest son, Suman Shastri, whose son, <a href="/wiki/Siddharth_Nath_Singh" class="mw-redirect" title="Siddharth Nath Singh">Siddharth Nath Singh</a> is a spokesman of the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> and Minister of Health, <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Uttar_Pradesh" title="Government of Uttar Pradesh">Government of Uttar Pradesh</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anil_Shastri" title="Anil Shastri">Anil Shastri</a> who is a member of his father's Congress Party, his son <a href="/wiki/Adarsh_Shastri" title="Adarsh Shastri">Adarsh Shastri</a> gave up his corporate career with <a href="/wiki/Apple_Inc" class="mw-redirect" title="Apple Inc">Apple Inc</a> to contest the <a href="/wiki/2014_Indian_general_election" title="2014 Indian general election">General elections of 2014</a> from <a href="/wiki/Allahabad" class="mw-redirect" title="Allahabad">Allahabad</a> on an <a href="/wiki/Aam_Aadmi_Party" title="Aam Aadmi Party">Aam Aadmi Party</a> ticket.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> He lost that election but was elected in 2015 as a member of the <a href="/wiki/Delhi_Legislative_Assembly" title="Delhi Legislative Assembly">Delhi Legislative Assembly</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Sunil_Shastri" title="Sunil Shastri">Sunil Shastri</a> who is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> and Ashok Shastri, the youngest son who worked in the corporate world before his death at the age of 37,<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> his wife Neera Shastri was a member of the <a href="/wiki/Bharatiya_Janata_Party" title="Bharatiya Janata Party">Bharatiya Janata Party</a> national executive.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Other members of the family, have also been involved in the corporate and social life of India.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="The text near this tag needs further explanation. (August 2021)">further explanation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Death">Death</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Death">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Mumbai_Shastri_statue.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Mumbai_Shastri_statue.jpg/220px-Mumbai_Shastri_statue.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="330" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Mumbai_Shastri_statue.jpg/330px-Mumbai_Shastri_statue.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b2/Mumbai_Shastri_statue.jpg/440px-Mumbai_Shastri_statue.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1260" data-file-height="1890" /></a><figcaption>Shastri's statue in <a href="/wiki/Mumbai" title="Mumbai">Mumbai</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Shastri died in Tashkent, Uzbekistan (then Soviet Union) on 11 January 1966, one day after signing a peace treaty to end the 1965 <a href="/wiki/Indo-Pakistani_War_of_1965" title="Indo-Pakistani War of 1965">Indo-Pakistan War</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> Many among Shastri's supporters and close relatives, refused at the time, and have refused since, to believe the circumstances of his death and allege foul play.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc.co.uk_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc.co.uk-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-hindustantimes.com_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hindustantimes.com-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> Conspiracy theories appeared within hours of his death and have thereafter persisted. He was eulogized as a national hero and the <a href="/wiki/Vijay_Ghat" class="mw-redirect" title="Vijay Ghat">Vijay Ghat</a> memorial established in his memory. Upon his death, <a href="/wiki/Gulzarilal_Nanda" title="Gulzarilal Nanda">Gulzarilal Nanda</a> once again assumed the role of acting prime minister until the <a href="/wiki/Indian_National_Congress" title="Indian National Congress">Congress Party</a> elected <a href="/wiki/Indira_Gandhi" title="Indira Gandhi">Indira Gandhi</a> over <a href="/wiki/Morarji_Desai" title="Morarji Desai">Morarji Desai</a> to officially succeed Shastri.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEU.N._Gupta2003121_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEU.N._Gupta2003121-65">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After Shastri's death, his wife <a href="/wiki/Lalita_Shastri" title="Lalita Shastri">Lalita Shastri</a> alleged he was poisoned. An epic poetry book in <a href="/wiki/Hindi" title="Hindi">Hindi</a> titled <i>Lalita Ke Aansoo</i><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKrant_M._L._Verma1978_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKrant_M._L._Verma1978-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> written by Krant M. L. Verma was published in 1978.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> In this book, the tragic story about the death of Shastri has been narrated by his wife Lalita.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> Journalist Gregory Douglas claimed to have conducted a series of interviews with CIA officer <a href="/wiki/Robert_Crowley_(CIA)" title="Robert Crowley (CIA)">Robert Crowley</a> in 1993. In a book published in 2013, 13 years after Crowley's death, Douglas wrote that Crowley euphemistically told him the CIA had assassinated Shastri, stating "And we nailed Shastri as well. Another cow-loving rag head", as well as Indian nuclear scientist <a href="/wiki/Homi_J._Bhabha" title="Homi J. Bhabha">Homi Bhabha</a> thirteen days later in order to thwart the <a href="/wiki/Indian_nuclear_programme" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian nuclear programme">Indian nuclear programme</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> The Indian media proceeded to report these claims largely unquestioned.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Indian Government released no information about his death and the media then was kept silent. The possible existence of a conspiracy was covered in India by the <a href="/wiki/Outlook_(Indian_magazine)" title="Outlook (Indian magazine)">'Outlook' magazine</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-outlookindia.com_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-outlookindia.com-72">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> A query was later posed by <a href="/wiki/Anuj_Dhar" title="Anuj Dhar">Anuj Dhar</a>, author of <i>CIA's Eye on South Asia</i>, under the <a href="/wiki/Right_to_Information_Act" class="mw-redirect" title="Right to Information Act">Right to Information Act</a> to declassify a document supposedly related to Shastri's death, but the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister%27s_Office_(India)" title="Prime Minister&#39;s Office (India)">Prime Minister's Office</a> refused to oblige, reportedly citing that this could lead to harming of foreign relations, cause disruption in the country and cause breach of parliamentary privileges.<sup id="cite_ref-hindustan_times_july11_2009_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hindustan_times_july11_2009-73">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> Another RTI plea by Kuldip Nayar was also declined, as PMO cited exemption from disclosure on the plea. The home ministry is yet to respond to queries whether India conducted a post-mortem on Shastri, and if the government had investigated allegations of foul play. The Delhi Police in their reply to an RTI application said they do not have any record pertaining to Shastri's death. The Ministry of External Affairs has already said no post-mortem was conducted in the USSR. The Central Public Information Officer of Delhi Police in his reply dated 29 July 2009<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> said, "No such record related to the death of the former prime minister of India Lal Bahadur Shastri is available in this district. Hence the requisite information pertaining to New Delhi district may please be treated as nil."<sup id="cite_ref-ndtv.com_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ndtv.com-75">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> This has created more doubts.<sup id="cite_ref-timesofindia.indiatimes.com_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-timesofindia.indiatimes.com-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> The death of Lal Bahadur Shastri is considered to be one of the biggest unsolved mysteries of Indian politics.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The PMO answered only two questions of the RTI application, saying it has only one classified document pertaining to the death of Shastri, which is exempted from disclosure under the RTI Act. It sent the rest of the questions to the Ministry of External Affairs and Home Ministry to answer. The MEA said the only document from the erstwhile Soviet Government is "the report of the Joint Medical Investigation conducted by a team comprising R. N. Chugh, Doctor in-Attendance to the PM and some Russian doctors" and added no post-mortem was conducted in the <a href="/wiki/USSR" class="mw-redirect" title="USSR">USSR</a>. The Home Ministry referred the matter to Delhi Police and National Archives for the response pertaining to any post-mortem conducted on the body of Shastri in India.<sup id="cite_ref-dnaindia.com_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-dnaindia.com-78">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="State_honours">State honours</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: State honours">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
