<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Category:Memorials_to_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri" title="Category:Memorials to Lal Bahadur Shastri">Category:Memorials to Lal Bahadur Shastri</a></div>
<p><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Narendra_Modi_offering_the_floral_tributes_on_the_statue_of_the_former_Prime_Minister,_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri,_at_the_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_National_Academy_of_Administration_(LBSNAA),_in_Mussoorie,_Uttarakhand.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Narendra_Modi_offering_the_floral_tributes_on_the_statue_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri%2C_at_the_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_National_Academy_of_Administration_%28LBSNAA%29%2C_in_Mussoorie%2C_Uttarakhand.jpg/240px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="240" height="172" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Narendra_Modi_offering_the_floral_tributes_on_the_statue_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri%2C_at_the_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_National_Academy_of_Administration_%28LBSNAA%29%2C_in_Mussoorie%2C_Uttarakhand.jpg/360px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/74/Narendra_Modi_offering_the_floral_tributes_on_the_statue_of_the_former_Prime_Minister%2C_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri%2C_at_the_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_National_Academy_of_Administration_%28LBSNAA%29%2C_in_Mussoorie%2C_Uttarakhand.jpg/480px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1440" data-file-height="1032" /></a></span>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial,_New_Delhi.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial%2C_New_Delhi.jpg/220px-Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial%2C_New_Delhi.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial%2C_New_Delhi.jpg/330px-Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial%2C_New_Delhi.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial%2C_New_Delhi.jpg/440px-Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial%2C_New_Delhi.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2576" data-file-height="1932" /></a><figcaption>Lal Bahadur Shastri Memorial, New Delhi</figcaption></figure>
<p>Shastri is now regarded as one of the most respected prime minister of India. He was a secularist who refused to mix religion with politics. In a public meeting held at the Ram Lila grounds in Delhi, a few days after the ceasefire, he complained about a <a href="/wiki/BBC" title="BBC">BBC</a> report which claimed that Shastri's identity as a Hindu meant that he was ready for a war with Pakistan. He stated:<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuha2008400–401_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuha2008400–401-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1062260506">.mw-parser-output .quotebox{background-color:#F9F9F9;border:1px solid #aaa;box-sizing:border-box;padding:10px;font-size:88%;max-width:100%}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatleft{margin:.5em 1.4em .8em 0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatright{margin:.5em 0 .8em 1.4em}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.centered{overflow:hidden;position:relative;margin:.5em auto .8em auto}.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatleft span,.mw-parser-output .quotebox.floatright span{font-style:inherit}.mw-parser-output .quotebox>blockquote{margin:0;padding:0;border-left:0;font-family:inherit;font-size:inherit}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-title{background-color:#F9F9F9;text-align:center;font-size:110%;font-weight:bold}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote>:first-child{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote:last-child>:last-child{margin-bottom:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote.quoted:before{font-family:"Times New Roman",serif;font-weight:bold;font-size:large;color:gray;content:" “ ";vertical-align:-45%;line-height:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox-quote.quoted:after{font-family:"Times New Roman",serif;font-weight:bold;font-size:large;color:gray;content:" ” ";line-height:0}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .left-aligned{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .right-aligned{text-align:right}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .center-aligned{text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .quotebox .quote-title,.mw-parser-output .quotebox .quotebox-quote{display:block}.mw-parser-output .quotebox cite{display:block;font-style:normal}@media screen and (max-width:640px){.mw-parser-output .quotebox{width:100%!important;margin:0 0 .8em!important;float:none!important}}</style><div class="quotebox pullquote floatright" style=";">
<blockquote class="quotebox-quote left-aligned" style="">
<p>While I am a Hindu, Mir Mushtaq who is presiding over this meeting is a Muslim. <a href="/wiki/Frank_Anthony" title="Frank Anthony">Mr. Frank Anthony</a> who has addressed you is a Christian. There are also Sikhs and Parsis here. The unique thing about our country is that we have Hindus, Muslims, Christians, Sikhs, Parsis, and people of all other religions. We have temples and mosques, <i>gurdwaras</i> and churches. But we do not bring all this into politics. This is the difference between India and Pakistan. Whereas Pakistan proclaims herself to be an Islamic State and uses religion as a political factor, we Indians have the freedom to follow whatever religion we may choose, and worship in any way we please. So far as politics is concerned, each of us is as much an Indian as the other.
</p>
</blockquote>
</div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Kuldip_Nayar" title="Kuldip Nayar">Kuldip Nayar</a>, Shastri's media advisor from 1960 to 1964, recalls that, during the <a href="/wiki/Quit_India_Movement" title="Quit India Movement">Quit India Movement</a>, his daughter was ill and he was released on parole from jail. However, he could not save her life because doctors had prescribed costly drugs. Later on in 1963, on the day when he was dropped from the cabinet, he was sitting in his home in the dark, without a light. When asked about the reason, he said as he no longer is a minister, all expenses will have to be paid by himself and that as an MP and minister he didn't earn enough to save for time of need.<sup id="cite_ref-The_politician_who_made_no_money_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-The_politician_who_made_no_money-81">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Although Shastri had been a cabinet minister for many years in the 1950s, he was poor when he died. All he owned at the end was an old car, which he had bought in instalments from the government and for which he still owed money. He was a member of Servants of India society (which included <a href="/wiki/Mahatma_Gandhi" title="Mahatma Gandhi">Mahatma Gandhi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lala_Lajpat_Rai" title="Lala Lajpat Rai">Lala Lajpat Rai</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gopal_Krishna_Gokhle" class="mw-redirect" title="Gopal Krishna Gokhle">Gopal Krishna Gokhle</a>) which asked all its members to shun accumulation of private property and remain in public life as servants of the people. He was the first railway minister who resigned from office following a major train accident as he felt moral responsibility.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGrover199323–29,_34Dhawan199198_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrover199323–29,_34Dhawan199198-82">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The foundation stone of <a href="/wiki/Bal_Vidya_Mandir,_Lucknow" title="Bal Vidya Mandir, Lucknow">Bal Vidya Mandir</a>, a distinguished school of <a href="/wiki/Lucknow" title="Lucknow">Lucknow</a>, was laid by him during his tenure as the prime minister, on 19 November 1964. He inaugurated the Central Institute of Technology Campus at Tharamani, Chennai, in November 1964.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDhawan1991_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDhawan1991-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> He inaugurated the Plutonium Reprocessing Plant at Trombay in 1965. As suggested by <a href="/wiki/Homi_J._Bhabha" title="Homi J. Bhabha">Dr. Homi Jehangir Bhabha</a>, Shastri authorized the development of nuclear explosives. Bhabha initiated the effort by setting up the nuclear explosive design group Study of Nuclear Explosions for Peaceful Purposes (SNEPP).<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> He inaugurated the Andhra Pradesh Agricultural University at Hyderabad on 20 March 1965 which was renamed the <a href="/wiki/Acharya_N._G._Ranga_Agricultural_University" title="Acharya N. G. Ranga Agricultural University">Acharya N. G. Ranga Agricultural University</a> in 1996 and was separated into two universities after the formation of Telangana State. The university in Telangana was named in July 2014 as <a href="/wiki/University_of_Selangor" title="University of Selangor">Professor Jayashanker Agricultural University</a>. Shastri also inaugurated the National Institute of Technology, <a href="/wiki/Allahabad" class="mw-redirect" title="Allahabad">Allahabad</a>. Lal Bahadur Shastri inaugurated the Jawahar Dock of the Chennai Port Trust and started the construction work of <a href="/wiki/V.O._Chidambaranar_Port_Authority" title="V.O. Chidambaranar Port Authority">V.O. Chidambaranar Port Authority</a> in November 1964.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> He inaugurated the Sainik School Balachadi, in the state of Gujarat. He laid the foundation stone of Almatti dam. The commissioned dam bears his name.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuha200867C.P._Srivastava199548_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuha200867C.P._Srivastava199548-86">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Shastri_Circle_Jodhpur.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Shastri_Circle_Jodhpur.jpg/220px-Shastri_Circle_Jodhpur.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Shastri_Circle_Jodhpur.jpg/330px-Shastri_Circle_Jodhpur.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Shastri_Circle_Jodhpur.jpg/440px-Shastri_Circle_Jodhpur.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6000" data-file-height="4000" /></a><figcaption>Shastri Circle Jodhpur</figcaption></figure>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Memorials">Memorials</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Memorials">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Category:Memorials_to_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri" title="Category:Memorials to Lal Bahadur Shastri">Category:Memorials to Lal Bahadur Shastri</a></div><figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_National_Academy_of_Administration,_Mussoorie,_at_night.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_National_Academy_of_Administration%2C_Mussoorie%2C_at_night.JPG/200px-Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_National_Academy_of_Administration%2C_Mussoorie%2C_at_night.JPG" decoding="async" width="200" height="150" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_National_Academy_of_Administration%2C_Mussoorie%2C_at_night.JPG/300px-Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_National_Academy_of_Administration%2C_Mussoorie%2C_at_night.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_National_Academy_of_Administration%2C_Mussoorie%2C_at_night.JPG/400px-Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_National_Academy_of_Administration%2C_Mussoorie%2C_at_night.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a><figcaption>Lal Bahadur Shastri National Academy of Administration, <a href="/wiki/Mussoorie" title="Mussoorie">Mussoorie</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial,_New_Delhi.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial%2C_New_Delhi.jpg/220px-Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial%2C_New_Delhi.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial%2C_New_Delhi.jpg/330px-Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial%2C_New_Delhi.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial%2C_New_Delhi.jpg/440px-Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Memorial%2C_New_Delhi.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2576" data-file-height="1932" /></a><figcaption>Lal Bahadur Shastri Memorial, New Delhi</figcaption></figure>
<p>Shastri was known for his honesty and humility throughout his life. He was posthumously awarded the <a href="/wiki/Bharat_Ratna" title="Bharat Ratna">Bharat Ratna</a>, and a memorial "<a href="/wiki/Raj_Ghat_and_associated_memorials" title="Raj Ghat and associated memorials">Vijay Ghat</a>" was built for him in Delhi. Several educational institutes including <a href="/wiki/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_National_Academy_of_Administration" title="Lal Bahadur Shastri National Academy of Administration">Lal Bahadur Shastri National Academy of Administration</a> (Mussorie, Uttarakhand) bear his name. The <a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Institute_of_Management&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Lal Bahadur Shastri Institute of Management (page does not exist)">Lal Bahadur Shastri Institute of Management</a> was established in Delhi by the Lal Bahadur Shastri Educational Trust in 1995. The Shastri Indo-Canadian Institute was named after Shastri due to his role in promoting scholarly activity between India and Canada.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> <i>Lal Bahadur Shastri Memorial</i> run by the Lal Bahadur Shastri National Memorial Trust, is situated next to <a href="/wiki/10_Janpath" class="mw-redirect" title="10 Janpath">10 Janpath</a> his residence when he was prime minister,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERajeshwar_Prasad199116_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERajeshwar_Prasad199116-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> at 1, Motilal Nehru Place, New Delhi.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> One of the halls of residence of <a href="/wiki/IIT_Kharagpur" title="IIT Kharagpur">IIT Kharagpur</a> is named after him as <i>Lal Bahadur Shastri Hall of Residence</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2011, on Shastri's 45th death anniversary, the Uttar Pradesh Government announced the renovation of Shastri's ancestral house at <a href="/wiki/Ramnagar,_Varanasi" title="Ramnagar, Varanasi">Ramnagar</a> in <a href="/wiki/Varanasi" title="Varanasi">Varanasi</a> and declared plans to convert it into a biographical museum.<sup id="cite_ref-hind_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hind-91">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Airport" title="Lal Bahadur Shastri Airport">Varanasi International Airport</a> is named after him.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> The Lal Bahadur Shastri Centre for Indian Culture with a monument and a street named after him are in the city of <a href="/wiki/Tashkent" title="Tashkent">Tashkent</a>, <a href="/wiki/Uzbekistan" title="Uzbekistan">Uzbekistan</a>. A few stadiums are named after him in the cities of <a href="/wiki/Hyderabad,_India" class="mw-redirect" title="Hyderabad, India">Hyderabad</a>, <a href="/wiki/Telangana" title="Telangana">Telangana</a>, Ahmedabad in Gujarat, Kollam in Kerala, Ghazhiabad and Bhawanipatna in Odisha. The Almatti Dam across the River Krishna in northern Karnataka was renamed the Lal Bahadur Shastri Sagar. The foundation stone was laid by him. MV Lal Bahadur Shastri, a cargo ship, is named after him. The Reserve Bank of India released coins in the denomination of 5 rupees during his birth century celebrations. An All India Lal Bahadur Shastri Hockey tournament has been held every year since 1991 – it is a major hockey tournament. The Left Bank Canal of the Nagarjuna Sagar Dam in Andhra Pradesh is named the Lal Bahadur Shastri Canal and is 295&#160;km in Length.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Some major roads in the cities of New Delhi, <a href="/wiki/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_Marg" title="Lal Bahadur Shastri Marg">Mumbai</a>, Pune, Puduchery, Lucknow, Warangal and Allahabad and Ernakulam are named after him, as is Sashtri Road, Kottayam, Kerala. There is a Lal Bahadur Shastri Medical College in <a href="/wiki/Mandi,_Himachal_Pradesh" title="Mandi, Himachal Pradesh">Mandi, Himachal Pradesh</a> and Shastri Bhavans in New Delhi, Chennai and Lucknow. In 2005, the Government of India created a chair in his honour in the field of democracy and governance at <a href="/wiki/Delhi_University" title="Delhi University">Delhi University</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-pmindia_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-pmindia-7">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Manmohan_Singh,_the_Speaker,_Lok_Sabha,_Shri_Somnath_Chatterjee_alongwith_other_dignitaries_paid_homage_to_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_on_his_104th_birth_anniversary,_at_Parliament_House,_in_New_Delhi.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Manmohan_Singh%2C_the_Speaker%2C_Lok_Sabha%2C_Shri_Somnath_Chatterjee_alongwith_other_dignitaries_paid_homage_to_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_on_his_104th_birth_anniversary%2C_at_Parliament_House%2C_in_New_Delhi.jpg/220px-thumbnail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Manmohan_Singh%2C_the_Speaker%2C_Lok_Sabha%2C_Shri_Somnath_Chatterjee_alongwith_other_dignitaries_paid_homage_to_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_on_his_104th_birth_anniversary%2C_at_Parliament_House%2C_in_New_Delhi.jpg/330px-thumbnail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Manmohan_Singh%2C_the_Speaker%2C_Lok_Sabha%2C_Shri_Somnath_Chatterjee_alongwith_other_dignitaries_paid_homage_to_the_former_Prime_Minister_Shri_Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_on_his_104th_birth_anniversary%2C_at_Parliament_House%2C_in_New_Delhi.jpg/440px-thumbnail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2100" data-file-height="1394" /></a><figcaption>Manmohan Singh, the Speaker, Lok Sabha, Shri Somnath Chatterjee along with other dignitaries paid homage to the former Prime Minister Shri Lal Bahadur Shastri on his 104th birth anniversary</figcaption></figure>
<p>A portrait of Shastri hangs in the Central Hall of the Parliament House of India. The portrait, painted by Vidya Bhushan, was unveiled by the then President of India, Dr. <a href="/wiki/Shanker_Dayal_Sharma" class="mw-redirect" title="Shanker Dayal Sharma">Shanker Dayal Sharma</a> on 2 October 1993.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_popular_culture">In popular culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: In popular culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Shastri's life and death, in particular, have been a subject of Indian popular culture. <i>Homage to Lal Bahadur Shastri</i> is a 1967 short documentary film directed by S. Sukhdev and produced by the <a href="/wiki/Films_Division_of_India" title="Films Division of India">Films Division of India</a> which pays tribute to the former prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> <i>Apne Shastri Ji</i> (1986) was also made as a homage to him.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><i><a href="/wiki/Jai_Jawaan_Jai_Kisaan_(film)" title="Jai Jawaan Jai Kisaan (film)">Jai Jawaan Jai Kisaan</a></i> is a 2015 Indian <a href="/wiki/Hindi" title="Hindi">Hindi</a>-language biographical drama film by Milan Ajmera, titled after the popular slogan by Shastri it portrays his entire life from birth to death where he is portrayed by Akhilesh Jain. <i>Lal Bahadur Shastri's Death</i>, a 2018 television documentary film by <a href="/wiki/Jyoti_Kapur_Das" title="Jyoti Kapur Das">Jyoti Kapur Das</a> reconstructs his death and covers various conspiracy theories around it, including interviews with his son <a href="/wiki/Sunil_Shastri" title="Sunil Shastri">Sunil Shastri</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> A film titled <i><a href="/wiki/The_Tashkent_Files" title="The Tashkent Files">The Tashkent Files</a></i> (2019), directed by <a href="/wiki/Vivek_Agnihotri" title="Vivek Agnihotri">Vivek Agnihotri</a> revolves around the mystery of the death of Lal Bahadur Shastri.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><i><a href="/wiki/Pradhanmantri" title="Pradhanmantri">Pradhanmantri</a></i> (<abbr style="font-size:85%" title="literal translation">lit.</abbr><span style="white-space: nowrap;">&#8201;</span>'Prime Minister&#39;), a 2013 Indian documentary television series which aired on <a href="/wiki/ABP_News" title="ABP News">ABP News</a> and covers the various policies and political tenures of Indian PMs, dedicated the entire seventh episode "Lal Bahadur Shastri" to his term as the country's leader with <a href="/wiki/Akhil_Mishra" title="Akhil Mishra">Akhil Mishra</a> in the role of Shastri.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 1967 film <i><a href="/wiki/Upkar" title="Upkar">Upkar</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/Manoj_Kumar" title="Manoj Kumar">Manoj Kumar</a>, which is based on the 1965 war, was dedicated to Shastri.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Lal_Bahadur_Shastri_(film)" title="Lal Bahadur Shastri (film)"><i>Lal Bahadur Shastri</i></a>, a 2014 Indian <a href="/wiki/Malayalam" title="Malayalam">Malayalam</a>-language comedy film by Rejishh Midhila is titled after the prime minister but has no apparent connection with his life.<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Citations">Citations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Lal_Bahadur_Shastri&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Citations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
